## iOS Metal optimization (2026-11-15, Mac simulator)

Publication scope: findings only. The Metal implementation, profiling script and
raw iOS evidence described below remain local and are not included in this
documentation commit. Reproduction requires those unpublished changes.

Owner: iOS Codex session. Base: `0331153`. Rebase from main before each
implementation step; preserve other agents' sections in this file.

User target: **60 FPS with substantial headroom**. User authorized a Metal-backed
iOS Painter and explicitly prioritized performance over current pixel accuracy.
Shared Java remains the only gameplay, layout, timing and scene implementation.

Measured on iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 simulator, full 3x backing resolution,
Release build. Frozen title, 30 warmup draws / 120 measured draw intervals:

- Original lazy PNG crops: 9.0 and 10.7 FPS, p95 intervals 166.8 / 133.4 ms.
- Materialized sprite frames (32 MiB CPU LRU): 14.8 / 15.0 FPS.
- Native BGRA frames and 8-bit UIKit layer: 20 FPS, 50.0 ms intervals.
- Direct Core Graphics bitmap: also 20 FPS. Not a useful default.
- These three optimized title paths matched all 3,162,132 captured native pixels
  in the frozen scene. This does not establish parity across all game states.
- Frozen tutorial: direct CG draw median 93.1 ms, p95 106.0 ms; UIKit draw
  submission median 51.4 ms plus deferred rasterization, ~10 FPS end-to-end.

Five-second sampling found PNG decoding during original display-list playback.
After materializing frames, sampling shifted to Core Graphics image sampling,
blending and repeated pixel-format conversion. This is host/simulator evidence,
not physical iPhone performance or a GPU-residency measurement.

Initial implementation step (superseded by the measured defaults below): native Metal Painter, retaining Core Graphics as
reference/resource rasterizer. Ordered batches, premultiplied-alpha textures,
80 MiB texture LRU, three in-flight vertex buffers, stencil-count shoreline
exclusion. Initial approximations: polygonal circles/round caps, linear texture
sampling and source-over sprite crossfades. No changes to shared simulation or
sprite selection. Performance and playable-output validation were pending at that stage; see final results below.

Raw runs live under ignored `out/ios-profile/`; script `ios/scripts/profile.py`.
Build with `CONFIGURATION=Release ios/scripts/build.sh` and explicit local Java /
J2ObjC paths if needed. Simulator installed build may be an intermediate capture.

Validation so far: shared `check.sh` passed (2816 assertions reported in its final
suite). No Android phone is connected to this Mac and no existing Barge Boss debug
signing key is present, so the usual Android install cannot be completed here.

### Metal prototype measurements

Apple Metal Toolchain 17F109 installed on this Mac; shaders are compiled by Xcode.
Full-resolution frozen title and tutorial now sustain 60 FPS. CPU draw median /
p95: title 4.24 / 5.78 ms, tutorial 4.44 / 4.67 ms. The first title capture overlapped
ending JVM previews and must be repeated for clean final evidence.

**Moving gameplay is not solved by that result.** The 120-warmup / 360-frame
`barge-arrival` shared replay fell to 8.8 FPS with the initial 80 MiB combined
texture LRU. CPU median/p95 116.6/173.2 ms while GPU median/p95 stayed 3.64/5.51 ms.
Sampling found repeated sprite PNG decoding and uploads. A working-set / eviction
instrumentation pass and controlled bounded-cache comparison are in progress.
Do not cite the frozen scene as evidence of 60 FPS during camera/heading changes.

### Follow-up: actual cache working set and headroom

The harbor replay references **110,601,912 bytes (~105.5 MiB) in one frame**.
The 80 MiB cache could not hold even a single frame's working set. A 128 MiB
cache improved the replay to 56.5 FPS but still had preparation spikes. Background
terrain preparation / neighboring-heading prefetch improved it to 59.0 FPS.
160 MiB bounded GPU storage eliminates measured eviction in this replay.

Current defaults: Metal; 160 MiB texture LRU; 32 MiB decoded-frame CPU LRU per
painter; 64 MiB shared decoded-source CPU LRU; 48 MiB static-raster CPU LRU per
painter. Main and serial preparation workers have separate painters; only the
immutable source-sheet cache is shared under a short lock, with decoding outside
the lock. GPU resources retained by in-flight commands can outlive LRU eviction.
Memory totals are **not** just the GPU LRU budget. Background queues are bounded
and publish immutable textures on the main thread. Source sheets remain CPU-only;
GPU sprite textures remain frame-sized. Circle quads use analytic shader coverage
rather than CPU tessellation. The simple source-over crossfade is still approximate.

After source-sheet caching, full-resolution 360-frame moving captures:

| Scene | Throughput | CPU median / p95 | GPU median / p95 | Worst interval |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Barge arrival | 59.66 FPS | 5.75 / 7.55 ms | 0.12 / 0.32 ms | 49.42 ms |
| Bridge transit | 59.84 FPS | 6.00 / 7.09 ms | 0.13 / 0.22 ms | 32.71 ms |

GPU timestamp values are simulator measurements, not physical-iPhone claims.
CPU/GPU are pipelined; do not add their stage times. Occasional cold preparation /
scheduling spikes remain: this is **not a claim of locked 60 FPS on every frame**.
A clean repeated capture and UI interaction validation remain before handoff.

Small shared change in this session: `Lettering.TextLayer.backgroundSafe()` now
returns true because paint reads only final text fields and immutable glyph rows.
This lets capable hosts prepare newly appearing text off the draw thread. No
shared gameplay or scene composition has changed.

### Final clean repeats (immutable text preparation enabled)

All builds and other checks were stopped before these runs. Each uses the same
shared replay, 120 warmup draws, and 360 measured draws at full 3x resolution.

| Capture | FPS | CPU median / p95 | GPU median / p95 | Max draw-start interval |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Harbor arrival 1 | 60.00 | 5.82 / 7.21 ms | 0.13 / 0.24 ms | 17.10 ms |
| Canal | 60.00 | 4.92 / 5.78 ms | 0.12 / 0.22 ms | 23.11 ms |
| Harbor arrival 2 | 60.00 | 5.69 / 7.69 ms | 0.16 / 0.26 ms | 20.49 ms |

Typical CPU margin is about 9–11 ms against a 16.7 ms budget. Rare long frames
remain, so this is measured 60 FPS throughput with p95 headroom, not a guarantee
that every cold load or every device stays below budget. Post-replay simulator
physical footprint was 288.5 MiB, peak 371.7 MiB (`sample`, separate from timing).
The baseline and final CSVs/metadata are retained in
`docs/performance/ios-metal-2026-11-15/`. Native screenshots stay untracked in
`out/ios-profile/`. The source cache and circle shader changes preserve gameplay;
visual approximations remain explicitly accepted for this performance round.

Reproduction:

```sh
CONFIGURATION=Release BARGEBOSS_IOS_PROFILE=1 ./ios/scripts/build.sh
# Install Release-iphonesimulator/BargeBoss.app on the booted simulator first.
python3 ios/scripts/profile.py arrival --scene=barge-arrival --replay --warmup=120 --frames=360
python3 ios/scripts/profile.py canal --scene=canal-middle --replay --warmup=120 --frames=360
# Reference backend, isolated saved progress, same capture mechanism:
python3 ios/scripts/profile.py reference --coregraphics --tutorial
```

The replay fixture classes are included only with `BARGEBOSS_IOS_PROFILE=1`.
A normal build omits them. Profiling does not load or save captain progress.
The GPU column reads the most recently completed command buffer, so it is a
stage distribution rather than a precisely paired per-row CPU/GPU trace. The
CPU column includes shared replay advance plus geometry/submission for Metal;
Core Graphics UIKit draw time excludes deferred display-list playback. Neither
CPU number measures final display scanout. Simulator vsync intervals are the
end-to-end pacing evidence here. Physical iPhone validation is still required.

## iOS agent — Android applicability after syncing 4451876

- Preserved both agents’ logs and both profiling guide sections when syncing main. The iOS measurements above precede the new shared wave/foam changes; they are not measurements of 4451876. Native rebuild/revalidation after this sync remains pending.
- Local shared `Lettering.TextLayer.backgroundSafe()` now returns true for immutable glyph layers. Android CanvasPainter already honors this flag for asynchronous preparation, so the change also enables Android background lettering preparation. Android timing benefit has not been measured.
- Android already has cropped frame caching (24 MiB), retained decoded source bitmaps, background terrain preparation, visible-work priority and per-frame texture/build/eviction counters. Do not duplicate the Core Graphics lazy-PNG fix or copy the Metal 160 MiB combined texture budget blindly.
- Possible follow-up: bounded adjacent-heading frame preparation if Android frameBuildNs or native upload traces show heading-change stalls. Android currently crops a missing frame synchronously. Prefetch must preserve shared frame selection and avoid evicting visible work; measure before adopting.
- Latest Android trace identifies native path work as the stronger target. The other agent’s proposed shared colorized wave stamps could replace repeated polygon preparation with cached sprite draws on both platforms. Keep world placement, frame/color selection and shore clipping in shared Java; no per-wave bitmap generation during drawing. This is a proposal, not implemented or measured.
- Metal-specific circle shaders do not directly transfer: Android already calls Canvas.drawCircle. A custom Android GPU painter would be a separate project, not a demonstrated requirement from these iOS results.

## 2026-11-16 01:15 UTC — Android agent: reviewed published iOS findings from 7404d76

- Pulled `7404d76`. Publication contains findings only: Metal implementation, iOS profiler, text-layer flag and raw iOS evidence remain unpublished. iOS measurements used base `0331153` and simulator hardware; they do not validate current Android/native performance or the later shared recipe. No runtime code changed in this review.
- Strong shared follow-up remains colorized/alpha wave stamps: one or two cached sprite draws can replace multiple path preparations. Keep source alpha, world placement, animation/frame/color selection and shore clipping in shared Java. Use a bounded atlas/color cache and test transparent overdraw; native recoloring must not allocate a bitmap for every wave every frame.
- **New Android evidence for cache-budget testing:** in each of `queue-baseline-final`, `queue-foam-1`, `queue-foam-2` under `docs/performance/render-queue-2026-11-15/`, the 24 MiB cropped-frame cache reached 23.980 MiB, performed 201 crops and evicted 29 entries during 360 measured draws. Frame-build p95 was 0.721–0.753 ms, max 1.214–1.596 ms. This confirms capacity pressure, not that every build was a repeated crop or that eviction caused the long waits. A controlled 24 → 32 MiB cropped-frame-cache comparison is a small, useful next experiment; keep source/layer/native texture storage separate in accounting. Do not copy Metal's 160 MiB combined GPU budget.
- Adjacent-heading preparation is technically transferable, but first check whether a larger bounded cache reduces rebuilds. Current `CanvasPainter.frameImage` creates missing cropped bitmaps synchronously; prefetch needs a bounded worker, visible-frame priority, immutable publication and no changes to shared heading selection. It does not by itself guarantee Android GPU upload is complete.
- `Lettering.TextLayer` is safe in principle to mark background-safe: paint reads final text/key fields and immutable decoded glyph rows. However Android currently builds only seven cached layers in these samples, max synchronous build 0.330–0.381 ms. `CanvasPainter.layer` immediately falls back to direct glyph drawing on an async miss, and the same four-job worker serves terrain. Therefore merely copying the flag may add temporary native drawing work/queue competition. Prefer ahead-of-use UI preparation if transition measurements justify it; this is lower priority than water paths/cache pressure.
- Already present on Android: materialized/cropped sprite frames, retained decoded source images, asynchronous terrain preparation and bounded layer/frame caches. Warm PNG decode time was zero in these samples. Do not reimplement the iOS lazy-PNG fix or replace our source cache with the iOS budget without measuring its different ownership/working set.
- Metal's analytic circle shader is not a drop-in change: Android already submits `Canvas.drawCircle` and the measured backend is Skia/Vulkan. A direct Android GPU Painter could bypass more path/state overhead, but is a separate backend project with clipping/order/blend/lifecycle work; the simulator Metal gains cannot be projected onto Android.
- Suggested sequence: (1) quick bounded 32 MiB cache A/B, (2) shared alpha/colorized wave-stamp prototype as the larger path-cost target, (3) heading prefetch if crop/upload stalls remain, (4) text preparation only where measured transitions justify it. Preserve the current `ad11a073:2:1350` baseline until a shared drawing change is deliberately made.

## 2026-11-16 — Android agent: full rotation preparation requested

- User requested a larger cache and explicit prefetch of **all** rotation frames, replacing the earlier adjacent-heading/32 MiB experiment plan. Inventory: 15 vessel/cargo/glow/shadow/surface-shading atlases, 1,024 cells, approximately 86 MiB. A 32 MiB cache cannot retain these complete sets.
- Implementing a 160 MiB cropped-cell CPU budget with complete rotation sets retained and other cells using the remaining LRU space. This is independently sized from Metal's combined GPU LRU; Android GPU residency remains driver-managed. Source sheets and the 48 MiB terrain/UI cache remain separately accounted.
- Preparation will run once per gameplay painter on a serial background worker, using immutable published crops and unchanged shared atlas bounds/pivots. Private terrain/review painters must not start redundant prefetchers. Keep demand fallback for immediate play, cancellation on close, diagnostic 24 MiB/no-prefetch baseline, and measure native cache behavior before claiming a frame-rate gain.

### Full rotation preparation — implementation and native review

- Implemented shared `SpriteAtlas.rotationAtlases()` hints, an explicit startup prefetcher on the Android gameplay painter, a 160 MiB cropped-cell budget, and pinning of every vessel/cargo rotation plus glow/shadow/all four surface-light directions. Private terrain painters do not launch prefetch. Demand requests still work during preparation; no gameplay/render ordering/registration changes.
- One background-priority worker crops one cell at a time. Atlas/source publication is concurrent and immutable; duplicate foreground/worker crops adopt the already-published bitmap without double-accounting. Decode/crop work happens outside the short LRU lock. A same-atlas request may wait for its in-progress decode. Close cancels preparation and rejects late publication. No bitmap is recycled while recorded drawing commands could reference it.
- Shared checks and instrumented APK build passed; replay remains `ad11a073:2:1350`. Native phone review prepared all 1,024 frames in **337.864 ms**, retaining **90,179,088 bytes (86.001 MiB)**. Compared 9,437,184 pixels covering sprite, affine and blend draws across every frame: zero differences against demand-loaded crops. A constrained 96 MiB diagnostic cache forced 27 unrelated evictions; revisiting every rotation caused zero rebuilds. Concurrent demand/preparation accounting, idempotent startup, and close/cancellation checks passed.
- Added debug cache-budget/prefetch switches and CSV preparation count/readiness/duration. Kept the older texture stress test explicitly at 24 MiB so it still exercises eviction after raising the production default. Matched moving replay measurements are next; the review timing is CPU preparation, not guaranteed GPU upload/residency or final frame pacing.

### Follow-up request — upcoming-level manifests

- The user now wants an explicit way to prefetch only assets needed by the upcoming level. Replacing the normal all-catalog startup with immutable shared `LevelAssets.Plan` manifests. Every included watercraft still expands into **all** headings and every glow/shadow/shading variant. The full-catalog method remains available to tooling/reviews.
- Challenge manifests derive from the same delivery-order catalog as actual feeder spawning, including later deliveries rather than just currently visible vessels. Tutorial has a complete coal-only rotation manifest covering its later towing/train phases. Selected challenge preparation starts from the menu; changing to practice or another challenge replaces the pending plan on the single worker. Old-only crops become ordinary LRU entries, preserving shared ones.
- Worker publication carries a generation so obsolete preparation cannot mark a newer plan ready or insert late results. Closing still rejects publication. Native follow-up will check unused cargo stays undecoded, rapid switches, dependency completeness and unpinning. Earlier all-catalog timing/capacity results remain useful historical evidence, but do not describe this narrower production policy.

Broadside/astern color separation, registered anchors, cargo ordering and shore clipping stay intact. The same visibility drives lamps and water reflections across all shared hosts.

A single rectangle-union geometry source supplies water masks, physical shore/dock collisions and foreground shoreline cutouts. Wooden dock spines connect to shore; vegetation sits inland and never participates in building fades. Removed ordinary industrial lamp reflections/sampling and unused shed-overlap work from this stage.

Actual generated color sheets are 1774×887. Hull registration uses one uniform scale plus translated pivots, preserving source alpha. Independent masks are generated ahead of gameplay. Waterline/foam uses the yacht's own opaque silhouette, not the barge mask.

Android cache budgets remain 160 MiB frames / 48 MiB terrain. Static grass/dock content uses immutable 400×280 local chunks, visibility culling and the existing asynchronous layer-preparation contract. Trees/rocks are culled independently. These changes avoid new per-frame image generation; native performance has not yet been measured for this stage, so no 60 FPS claim.

No position snapping or scripted propulsion was needed. Full checks/art/native validation in progress.

### Montrose geometry audit — raised depth and native clip union

- Shared geometry: confirmed the zero-distance wall-contact normal fix against clockwise/counterclockwise polygons, both inside/outside constraints, tug/yacht hulls and all four edges. Android/JVM union clipping hid this issue; iOS even-odd holes could reopen them. Shore masks now return disjoint pieces. Original source triangles are the bounded blockers, with bounding-box rejection and tiny/zero-area pruning. An initial cut-fragment-as-blocker experiment was discarded because it caused unnecessary subdivision. No new source assets or per-frame raster textures.
- Stabilized polygon signed area using origin-relative double accumulation; absolute float shoelace cancellation could reverse thin fragment orientation. Edge output arrays now tolerate extra numerical crossings instead of throwing. Output geometry remains shared float coordinates.

This is a correctness cost, not an optimization claim: maintain tight per-vessel regions and profile broad foam masks before widening them. Fixed-step/render rules and native clip contract take precedence over fewer draw commands.

### Montrose water clipping candidate — exact output preserved, runtime adoption rejected

- Investigated the new marina `paintWater` path after geometry stabilized at **78 water triangles / 330 dock polygons**, including the north service floats. A separate experimental clipper uses immutable 128-unit spatial bins and thread-local candidate arrays, preserves original triangle/dock order, and directly passes polygons only when their complete bounds lie strictly inside one original triangle with no overlapping dock. Large/off-grid shapes retain the original clipping/subtraction sequence. This is a candidate, **not delegated by MontroseHarbor.paintWater**.
- Exact-output review covered **7,692** deterministic convex stamps at coast vertices, dock corners, triangle boundaries, bin boundaries, ordinary water/land, and large/out-of-grid fallbacks. Both iterations matched **28,740 ordered output polygons / 283,344 float coordinates bit-for-bit**, plus **1,440,000 Java2D pixels**. This proves equivalence for those samples, not universal geometry proof or native Android/iOS raster parity. Review source is `tools/MontroseWaterClipReview.java`; raw images and measurements are under `out/montrose-water-clip-review/` and `out/montrose-water-clip-review-v2/`.
- The index retained **660 cells / 5,196 shape references / 154 strictly interior triangle cells**, with **23,424 bytes integer payload** (array headers, source bounds and per-thread buffers are additional). Lazy initialization measured 4.91 ms first candidate and 3.88 ms focused follow-up on this Termux JVM. No giant texture or per-frame index build was introduced.
- Timing uses the same **6,154** small-water-stamp CPU fixture batch, four warmup passes, six alternating baseline-first/indexed-first pairs, and no pixel/trace-capture work. The first candidate's median was **18.81 ms baseline / 27.83 ms indexed**. One focused follow-up replaced repeated single-bin candidate insertion with array copies and added a strict interior-bounds pass-through: median **19.48 ms baseline / 23.83 ms indexed**, ranges **11.37–36.53 / 7.93–33.13 ms**. These noisy JVM microbenchmarks include allocation/GC effects; they are not native frame intervals. They do **not** establish an effective improvement.
- **Decision: keep the existing runtime clipper.** Do not add the experimental index to production or normal preview startup merely because it scans fewer candidates. Preserve the experiment for targeted future native profiling, especially allocation and clip arithmetic costs. No 60 FPS or presentation improvement claim. Root's graphical integration/install remains independent of this rejected optimization.
- The rejected candidate lives only in `tools/MontroseWaterClip.java`, outside every automatic shared/native source discovery path. Manual reproduction (isolated output): `mkdir -p build/montrose-water-experiment`; `rg --files core > build/montrose-water-experiment/sources.txt`; `javac --release 8 -d build/montrose-water-experiment @build/montrose-water-experiment/sources.txt desktop/com/bargeboss/game/DesktopPainter.java tools/MontroseWaterClip.java tools/MontroseWaterClipReview.java`; `java -Xmx512m -Djava.awt.headless=true -cp build/montrose-water-experiment com.bargeboss.game.MontroseWaterClipReview out/montrose-water-clip-manual`. It is intentionally absent from normal preview/build checks.

### Montrose performance regression investigation — native baseline

- User reported poor frame rate after the marina update and requested twice-closer
  default zoom. Added opt-in `montrose-inlet`, `montrose-docks`, and
  `montrose-overview` profile fixtures; non-tutorial profile rows use lesson -1.

  camera target. Physics and shared input remain the production implementation.
- Android frozen inlet baseline (60 warmup +120 measured frames),
  `out/profiles/20261115-215003-montrose-inlet-freeze`: UI draw median20.125ms,
  p95 22.424ms; draw interval median44.982ms, p95 54.449ms. Every measured frame
  has18 terrain fallbacks:2160 async misses total,367 background builds, but only
 6.424MiB retained layers.347 affine draws,2658 polygons and321 clips per frame.
- Found wrong preparation hints: Montrose passes an already quantized raster
  density to `prepareLayer`, whose host quantizes it again. A display scale
  needing2x gets a3x speculative job. The per-draw request still wants2x, leaving
  repeated expensive direct drawing and unnecessary worker jobs. Agent is fixing
  scale hints; native follow-up will establish the effect before claiming gains.
- Raised-shore masks also repeatedly scan static edges and allocate bounds;
  separate agent is preparing exact geometry precomputation/reuse. Baseline APK
  was completed before those changes; comparisons will name each included fix.

### Montrose shoreline construction — exact geometry reuse after performance report

- Shared JVM/runtime: each old shore request rebuilt normals for roughly 1,300 immutable edges and allocated bounds for every dock, including small buoy/rope masks. Camera-facing edge geometry/bounds are now prepared once; projection reuses existing land/dock/water bounds. A primitive float accumulator replaces boxed `Float` entries without changing triangle order or arithmetic.
- Added a per-thread exact mask cache keyed by the immutable camera object plus world region and physical depth. It retains at most **32 entries / 65,536 floats (256 KiB float payload)**, evicts within that bound, and clears when camera identity changes. Region/depth changes cannot reuse stale results. This targets repeated surface/waterline or hull requests in the same frame; it does not quantize cameras, geometry or motion. Cached arrays are immutable/borrowed, matching the painter input contract. Masks larger than the payload limit are computed but not retained.
- Evidence: **180 varied masks / 207,648 float coordinates** match the saved pre-change implementation bit-for-bit. Actual Java2D exclusion clips matched **23,100,000 pixels across 30 scenes** (five locations, three zooms, surface and foreground-depth passes), with **zero differences**. `MontroseGeometryTest` now passes **4,748 assertions**, adding exact reuse, region/depth/camera invalidation, rebuilt-coordinate equality and 32-entry eviction checks to existing collision/depth/native union coverage.
- Construction-only matched JVM medians, baseline → candidate: entrance **0.127 → 0.018 ms**; slip **0.320 → 0.207 ms**; dense O-dock **0.640 → 0.492 ms**. Repeated exact requests measured roughly **0.0002–0.00035 ms**. A broad 650-world-unit radius remains expensive on a cache miss (**10.325 → 10.061 ms**; candidate p95 **24.892 ms**), while its repeated request measured about **0.006 ms**. These are isolated geometry measurements, not Android frame intervals; root is capturing the native baseline/candidate separately.
- Kept a second experiment only under untracked `out/`: retaining whole convex dock/wall quads until union reduced the broad-case median in a separate run, but changed **17,192 of 23.1 million reviewed clip pixels**. It was **not adopted**. Runtime remains the bit-identical precomputed-edge/cache version. Do not infer visual equivalence from fewer polygons or a faster union. Review sources/results are `out/MontroseShoreOptimizationReview.java`, `out/MontroseShoreCachePixelReview.java` and the isolated rejected quad review.

### Montrose static-layer audit and bounded raster correction

- Read-only inspection found a concrete prepare-key mismatch: MontroseView and MarinaPedestals supplied `StaticLayer.rasterScale(displayScale)` to `prepareLayer`, while CanvasPainter performs that quantization itself. A requested density2 became density3 in the worker. `TerrainRasterizer.beginFrame` clears prior visibility; mismatched speculative jobs can evict completed correct-density jobs before the subsequent actual layer draw takes them. The native inlet baseline `out/profiles/20261115-215003-montrose-inlet-freeze/frames.csv` had **18 async misses/fallbacks every sampled frame**, **367 background builds**, only **6.424 MiB retained layers**, and roughly **7.57–9.93 MiB pending**. These counters are consistent with the concrete key mismatch, rather than a need to increase the cache budget.
- Corrected only preparation arguments: terrain/pool hints now carry actual display scale; halo hints carry their actual minimum/maximum breathing scales and request both only if host density differs. Layer drawing scales, source artwork, geometry and animation are unchanged. New `MarinaLayerPreparationTest` verifies every background-safe layer draw is covered by its host-quantized hint, including 1.6 and halo thresholds, unchanged prepared/nonprepared command traces, nonempty bounded chunks, local working-set budget and render purity. Final isolated run: **2,388 assertions passed**. Root registered the test and owns native timing comparisons; its first candidate also includes shore-mask caching, so total timing gains should not be attributed solely to this argument correction.
- Measured all **357** original Ground keys: **357 unique**, so key collisions were not the problem. At a 1333×600 old inlet camera, **25** submitted 400×310 chunks demanded **47.30 MiB** at density2 before other layers; **9 were completely transparent**, and raster alpha coverage was **11.27%**. Old docking camera: **13** chunks demanded **55.34 MiB** at density3 against the shared **48 MiB** limit, with only **7.80%** alpha coverage. Review-only full-harbor camera submitted **336** chunks / **635.74 MiB**. A cache cannot retain these complete visible working sets. Construction-only JVM medians for expanding original chunk commands into a no-op painter were 3.94 ms inlet, 3.48 ms docks and 23.30 ms overview; these exclude native raster work and are not frame intervals.
- Ground now precomputes conservative projected content bounds once from land triangles intersecting its world tile, the exact front-facing clipped seawall ranges, and every corner of its owned dock pieces at ground/deck heights. **132 empty chunks** are skipped. Original 400×310 camera/material clips and command order remain; a translation wrapper compensates cropped origin at paint time and the scene compensates the same origin at draw time. Four source pixels preserve filtered material fringes/AA/cap strokes; bounds clamp to the original viewport. No source image edits, new textures, per-frame geometry recording, cache-budget increase or platform-specific layout.
- Compared all **357** old/new Ground rasters against baseline source from commit `4b6237cfddc05f9066cc06c4e55161f81455667c`, **44,268,000 pixels** total. Initial2px bounds lost159 edge pixels; expanded4px bounds passed with **zero baseline alpha pixels outside the new crop**. Integer-origin retranslation produces small AA differences: **23,604 changed pixels (0.0533%)**, **5,787 with RGBA difference sum>60 (0.0131%)**, mean channel error **0.00812/255**. **746 pixels** inside preserved bounds changed from alpha>8 to0 due to boundary resampling; no source geometry was removed. This is not a pixel-identical claim. Representative before/after tiles were visually inspected; raw legacy source, review program, tile PNGs and logs are under `out/montrose-layer-audit/`.
- Tight working-set projection at the same old cameras: inlet **10 chunks / 8.61 MiB**, docks **9 / 15.06 MiB**. At the separately requested2x closer cameras: inlet **3 / 3.97 MiB**, docks **3 / 4.74 MiB**. Full-harbor debug overview still needs **214 chunks / 224.38 MiB** at current density2 and remains an unresolved wide-view limitation; do not claim this round fixes every overview or reaches60FPS. Root will measure final native frame intervals with closer-camera changes labeled separately.

### Wake motion correction after visual feedback (2026-11-16)

- The previous V-arm implementation inflated each entire V with age and moved its
  apex astern. Segment lengths grew continuously, while per-segment alpha and
  shader phase changed independently. This made the wake read as stretching,
  pulsing graphics rather than crests propagating through water.
- Each packet now emits two fixed-length wavelets. Every point on an arm moves
  at the same normal velocity: 0.24 units/tick along the recorded travel direction
  and 0.32 units/tick sideways. A moving emitter creates the V envelope from its
  history; individual packets do not inflate or follow later turns. Eight-tick
  fade-in removes abrupt births; registered crest texture no longer boils or
  marches independently. Small segment overlap softens repeated patch seams.
- Detached shoulder foam holds its material phase. Prop-wash material rolls
  slowly with particle age (0.006 radians/tick) and decal rotation falls from
  0.006 to 0.0015 radians/tick. Strong arcade coverage, lifetimes, emission counts,
  pool bounds, water-space drift and all gameplay wake forces are preserved.
  This is a visual wave model, not a new fluid simulation or buoy-force model.
- Added temporal tests for fixed segment dimensions, identical displacement of
  every point, stable material phase and continued propagation after source
  turn/stop: 1,632 assertions over all headings. Updated the older bounds test to
  verify the actual constant separation speed instead of requiring an expanding
  graphic. Full shared suite and cargo derivative checks pass; JVM/J2ObjC parity
  is `6f97e6d:2:1350`. Foam shoreline/alpha (197,334) and all-heading production
  hull/cargo shoreline (1,439,937) pixel checks pass.
- Reviewed the five-age propagation strip and sampled frames from a native iOS
  motion recording through a canal turn. Local untracked review artifacts:
  `out/wake-motion/propagation.png`, `native-motion.mp4`, `native-motion-frames.png`.
  Video recording was separate from performance measurements. Release simulator
  install and unsigned Android build succeeded. Android phone was not used;

- Isolated iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 Release Metal canal-middle A/B, 60 warmup and
  300 measured frames: 59.61/60.00 FPS; interval p95 17.258/17.267 ms; CPU draw

  shading, and shared water-level inputs. Existing effect patches do not provide
  this surface-mapping contract. Avoid screen-space noise that swims as parts move.

  active texture/cache cost. Before enabling them, crop retained frames, declare
  assets in shared manifests, bound caches, verify foreground shore coverage and
  compare native frames/timing. Alpha/image audits are not native runtime
  validation. No Android phone use in this asset round.

- Installed the industrial bollard, loading, truck and steelworks source frames with

  Java 2D/Core Graphics use opaque-color fallbacks, not equivalent texture detail.

- Native CPU sampling after the v2 run identifies the remaining cost more precisely:

  This is sustained native CPU preparation/submission cost, not predominantly sleep.
- Buffer dequeue median/p95 0.005/0.013 ms, but maximum 16.597 ms. Six explicit
  buffer-release waits and FrameTimeline buffer-stuffing/resync flags identify
  intermittent pacing pressure, not the dominant flush cost.

  waits cannot attribute overlapping water/foam/light shader cost. Do not claim
  GPU overdraw as proven. Controlled effect-family comparison is the next step.

  under out/android-profile/perfetto-montrose-v1. Normal app restored, timeout reset.

## 2026-11-16: moored geometric normal maps and distinct impact spray

- Baked true world-space face normals from all six rigid NPC models at 32 headings.

  lights; boats remain dark base silhouettes with no lamp emission. Shared Java
  owns light selection, direction, energy, headings and blending. The existing
  three RGB passes remain bounded; no per-frame lit image generation on mobile.
- Added a 192-cell, 128px normal atlas: +12 MiB source data and +12 MiB prepared
  frames (Android measured source 102.524→114.524 MiB, frames 96.521→108.521 MiB).
  The input BitmapShader LRU is limited to 64 frames; native frame caches keep
  their existing limit. Metal vertices carry an additional direction vector.
- A native visual check caught missing forwarding in `SceneryOcclusion`; the
  first attempted after capture was still flat RGB and is excluded from results.
  `NormalBoatReview` now tests the complete scene wrapper path, 192 exact alpha
  registrations and 34,554 interior unit normals. Local-coordinate baking removes
  antialias rounding differences between atlas rows.
- Pixel 11 Pro, Montrose docks, same replay ticks 2596–2895, 300 samples after 60
  warmup: baseline median/p95 draw 12.265/15.378 ms, frame interval 19.136/34.483 ms,

  interval 19.948/42.735 ms, 41.83 FPS. Issue-to-swap median 19.894→20.789 ms;
  this includes driver/wait time, not isolated CPU/GPU attribution. 60 FPS with
  headroom remains unresolved. See `docs/performance/moored-normals-2026-11-16`.
- Directional lighting uses one weighted direction per color at each boat center,
  with effective 12-unit source elevation. It is diffuse shading, without

  cancel direction. Pre-API-33 Android and Core Graphics retain flat RGB fallback;
  Java2D is a slow reference raster for visual review. iOS validation is simulator
  Metal only; it is not physical-iPhone performance evidence.
- Collision spray no longer reuses propwash whirls: new shared shader modes produce
  tapered spray fingers and expanding broken foam crowns, with detached ballistic
  droplets. Heavy-hit sparks remain. Existing fixed pools, timing, physical
  impulses and shore/depth scopes remain unchanged. Added droplets cost up to
  three circle draws per visible packet; this run does not establish the peak
  cost of all 24 simultaneous contact slots.
- Validation: full `check.sh`, JVM/J2ObjC parity `504d4681:2:1350`, 1,439,937
  all-heading shore pixel checks and 197,334 foam/splash pixel checks pass.
  Android and iOS Metal compile; Android hardware and simulator normal-map views

- Corrected normal-map repeat: draw median/p95 12.754/15.990 ms, frame interval

  versus the baseline; frame interval tails vary substantially. No frame-texture

  feature cost, not evidence of performance headroom or a pure GPU measurement.

## 2026-11-16: stamped splash revision and broader guidance

- Replaced procedural collision spray fingers/crowns, round droplets and radial
  flashes with four irregular transparent splash textures. Three airborne stamps
  and one expanding surface stamp per contact reuse the existing affine sprite
  path. Stable frame/orientation per packet avoids time-based atlas popping and
  velocity-direction flips at the top of the ballistic arc. Heavy-hit sparks stay.
- Runtime atlas is 256×256 RGBA with four 128px padded cells: 256 KiB decoded,
  at most another 256 KiB for cropped frames. Every level's shared preparation
  manifest includes it. The full generated authoring PNG lives under docs, so it
  is not bundled or decoded by the game. No new shader or per-frame image work.
- The prior six spray patches plus 18 round droplets and early radial flash are
  replaced by three airborne sprite submissions. Pool/physics stay bounded and
  unchanged. This reduces splash submission counts; it is not a measured FPS gain.
- Guide lines, arrows, elliptical rings and slip corners have broader soft color
  bands and bright central ridges, with a shared simulation-clock pulse. Existing
  three-pass stroke/ring budgets are preserved. Wider coverage increases overdraw;
  no claim of a free GPU change. Destination/gate arrowheads are constructed in the
  game plane rather than upright screen coordinates. Stroke glow remains bounded
  at close zoom. The unchanged quiet-cue tests were updated for the requested
  strong centers while retaining translucent feather and zoom bounds.
- Added a deterministic `splash-impact` developer fixture using an actual vessel
  collision for repeatable native review. Four contact-motion desktop sequences,
  the stamped shoreline pixel regression, and the broader Montrose guide view were
  reviewed. Native/build validation is recorded below when complete.
- Final validation: full `check.sh`, 197,334 foam/shore pixel assertions, 27 debug

  simulator builds succeed. Actual Metal and Android collision fixtures plus
  Android Montrose guidance were inspected; no physical-iPhone validation.
- One 300-frame Montrose replay sanity capture (60 warmup, same ticks 2596–2895):
  draw median/p95 13.205/15.764 ms, intervals 19.604/33.237 ms, effective 43.58 FPS.

## 2026-11-16: independent moored-boat point lights

- Removed RGB-weighted averaged directions from the production receiver path.
  Up to four strongest nearby lamps retain independent position, physical height,
  radius, energy and color. Surface direction and distance are evaluated per pixel,
  so low pedestals illuminate nearby faces and decks rather than acting overhead.
- Packed octahedral normal RG plus physical height B into the existing 128px cells.
  Surface XYZ is reconstructed through the inverse shared projection. No extra
  texture, bitmap cache or render target; cropped storage remains 108.771 MiB.
  Android AGSL and Metal use the same `boat-surface-light.inc`. Desktop is a slow
  CPU reference; pre-API33/software/Core Graphics retain an approximate RGB fallback.
- Bounded four-light selection uses reusable thread-local scratch and an early
  spatial reject. It replaces three aggregate color passes with up to four actual
  light passes per heading, so it is a correctness change, not a claimed speedup.
- Full checks pass, including 192 exact alpha registrations, 117,310 surface-height
  samples, opposing-source retention, back-facing rejection, distance falloff and
  wrapper forwarding. JVM/J2ObjC parity is `1cb53695:2:1350`. Android device and Metal
  simulator builds/pixels inspected. No physical iPhone validation.
- A 300-frame Montrose sanity capture: draw median/p95 14.863/17.265 ms;
  interval median/p95 20.866/33.781 ms, no source/frame builds during samples.
  The earlier capture had substantially slower simulation as well as rendering,
  so these runs are not a controlled performance comparison. Summaries under
  `docs/performance/point-lights-2026-11-16`; raw CSVs retained in ignored `out/`.
  Still not locked 60 FPS. Four-source cutoff, center-tested navigation sectors,
  no cabin self-shadow maps, and filtered normal seams remain approximations.

## 2026-11-16: saturated arcade guides and planar arrow ordering

- Replaced pale/white cue cores with saturated yellow, cyan, green, red and violet
  families. Guide core widths are doubled (5.6–9 reference units before zoom
  compensation). Rings and target borders are segmented, with empty centers,
  same-color shoulders/glow and simulation-clock breathing. Montrose's slip is a
  full dashed rectangle rather than dock-like isolated corner strokes.
- Filled arrows project all corners and feathering in the water plane. A bounded
  16-arrow thread-local queue collects existing shared cue rules against a no-op
  painter and merges with watercraft/scenery in both depth sorters. The later
  overlay pass suppresses duplicates. No gameplay mutation or wall-clock animation.
  Current sorting uses arrow midpoint, not per-fragment depth; very long arrows
  spanning multiple hull depths would need subdivision. The cue prepass has CPU
  cost and must be measured, not described as free.
- Shared `effects.inc` has single-patch arrow and dashed-line materials on AGSL
  and Metal. A long border no longer requires three native strokes per dash;
  dashed rings retain three bounded material passes. No new textures or render
  targets. Reference/older painters use projected polygons and segmented strokes;
  their feathering differs from shader pixels.
- `SceneCueTest` now verifies saturated palettes, doubled/bounded widths, planar
  arrows ahead of and behind the tug in both environments, and cleanup after a
  frame. Full checks pass; 1,439,937 all-heading hull/cargo shoreline assertions
  and 197,334 foam/splash assertions pass. JVM/J2ObjC parity `a13d1f49:2:1350`.
  Android and iOS Metal builds pass; final Metal simulator pixels reviewed.
  Android installed/launched successfully, but the phone changed to another app
  before a final visual/profile check completed. The overwritten capture contains
  zero sample frames and is rejected; do not reuse the prior lighting timings as
  neon-guide results. Final Android pixel/timing validation remains pending.
- Added `docs/art-direction/gameplay-highlights.md`, linked from `AGENTS.md` and
  the drawing-order contract. It cites verified Valve readability guidance and
  separates those principles from the user's Barge Boss arcade style decisions.

  14 logical points of travel; steering/neutral remain relative to that touchdown.
  After selection, the existing seven-point neutral zone and gesture-long gear
  latch remain. Direct tug drag retains its existing 42-world-unit threshold.

  with off-center landing on the wrong side, opposite initial jitter, subthreshold
  motion, intended drag, no unintended spin and returning to actual neutral.
  Confirmed these tests fail on the old Joystick at off-center touchdown.
- Waterline now uses one translucent strip instead of 24 separately alpha-blended
  capped strokes. The same color/alpha and occlusion curve remain; internal joins
  no longer accumulate opacity into visible dots. Adds one bounded 400-byte strip
  per active vessel and reduces 24 line submissions to one polygon. No measured
  performance claim. 236 actual alpha probes, including joins, all equal 72/255.
- Final validation: 1,038 drive-direction and 107 joystick assertions pass,
  including the new wrong-way touchdown regression and existing public tutorial.
  Full shared checks, Android/Metal builds and JVM/J2ObjC parity

## 2026-11-17: lower engine pitch and suppress intrusive water/high frequencies

- User's first phone audition found intrusive splashing and a high idle. Added
  offline sixth-order zero-phase low-pass filtering before loop seam construction:
  500 Hz for marine idle, 1000 Hz for truck working loop. No runtime filter cost.
  Marine 50–250 Hz power is unchanged to measurement precision; 1.2–8 kHz power
  falls about 59.7 dB. This is spectral suppression, not perfect water isolation.
- Shared playback-rate ranges lowered from .86–1.10 to .72–.92 (marine) and
  .78–1.12 to .68–.96 (truck), roughly 2–3 semitones below the first integration.
  Gains, load crossfade, lifecycle and source provenance stay unchanged. Same
  frame counts and decoded memory budget; originals remain intact in studies.
- Further user report identified a splash-like sound about every two seconds
  underway. The old truck excerpt spanned a rev decay and another attack inside
  its 1.84-second loop: 100 ms RMS max/min ratio 4.74. Replaced it with the
  sustained 3.55–4.35-second source plateau and offline 60 ms envelope leveling,

## 2026-11-20 — Android Vulkan experiment: GPU headroom, no throughput win

- Implemented an opt-in Vulkan 1.0/arm64 Painter with the existing shared Java
  scene, shader sources, cropped assets and Canvas resource preparation. Normal
  launches/builds remain Canvas. Unsupported native initialization/drawing falls
  back; cold translucent groups use Canvas preparation frames to preserve group
  opacity. iOS unchanged.
- Added bounded retained surface commands (96 chunks / 8 MiB), deferred texture
  transfer retirement, conservative per-scope stencil scissors, transient S8
  stencil, state-bind deduplication and compatible shader-family batching.
  Bounded stencil improved the exploratory single-sample interval 42.35 ->
  33.71 ms with zero changed pixels across 2,602,800 native screenshot pixels.
  4x MSAA and specialization per individual shader kind were rejected for cost.
  Single-sample edge quality remains an experimental-backend limitation.
- GPU timestamps exposed only ~4.3–4.6 ms actual GPU work; presentation includes
  significant driver CPU (~8.6–10 ms), plus waits. A bounded native render thread
  prevents UI blocking, but replacing queued frames is not an FPS improvement.
  Added a per-frame Vulkan submission marker and a capture tool that retains
  compositor timestamps, native CPU/GPU timings and UI CSVs together.
- Final V/C/C/V sequence (240 warmup + 600 UI samples each): mean displayed
  intervals Vulkan 22.69 / 27.93 ms; Canvas 22.56 / 22.29 ms. Compositor history
  contains the final 62 intervals per run. No repeatable Vulkan throughput win;
  no 60 FPS/headroom claim. UI drawing alone misleadingly reached ~11 ms in
  Vulkan A. Thermal status LIGHT; second-pass degradation not isolated.
- No larger cache justified: sampled frame construction counts were zero, with
  a stable 3.90 MiB background reservation. GPU texture budget is 160 MiB in
  addition to CPU bitmap ownership, not proof of physical residency.
- Shared all-heading shoreline regressions and full Android checks/build passed;
  native scoped-stencil pixels matched and final tutorial frame was inspected.
  Complete Vulkan-native 32-heading, cache-pressure, all-stage and cross-vendor
  validation remain outstanding. Keep this backend experimental.
- Raw evidence, reproducible commands, memory limits, rejected approaches and
  rollout limitations: docs/performance/vulkan-2026-11-20/README.md.
- Normal title launch additionally exposed duplicate GPU uploads of a shared sky
  bitmap under different frame keys. Fixed with bitmap-identity texture sharing,
  retaining strip UVs and the 160 MiB budget; no cache enlargement. Full checks
  and Android build passed again after this correction.
- After sharing the sky texture, normal title launch stayed on Vulkan, and a
  background/foreground surface recreation passed without fallback. Returned
  the development phone to normal Canvas launch after validation.

## 2026-11-20 Android thin wave zoom geometry
- Investigated reported wave disappearance at some zoom levels. Vulkan's float
  absolute-coordinate winding sum loses precision for tiny projected polygons,
  sometimes rejecting every ear. Use relative coordinates and double accumulation
  for winding only; shared geometry, density, clipping and vertex colors unchanged.
- Regression failed on prior encoder at scale 0.1; all 722 zoom/winding cases pass
  with the fix. Existing 2,526,477-field encoder golden and clip checks pass, as

  No performance improvement claimed. Installed final APK. Single-sample subpixel

  zoom shimmer is resolved. Evidence: docs/performance/wave-zoom-2026-11-20/.

- Slip success now covers the full authoritative slip rectangle once the tug

  longer prevent tapping the instructor. Normal location gameplay is unchanged.
- First helm exercise now requires gentle forward throttle (up to 45%) then
  stronger throttle (at least 75%), with real travel for both. Reads the same
  normalized throttle as the HUD and preserves drag-tug and left/right joystick
  controls. No new assets, caches or rendering primitives.

  score totals are unchanged.

## 2026-11-21 — 64-pose fleet and level residency (Android/shared, in progress)

The interrupted equipment capture reached 159.98 MiB of the 160 MiB CPU cropped
frame cache. Code inspection found a failure path: pinned rotations leave no
space for scenery crops; demand draws can return uncached fresh bitmaps. Vulkan
then sees changed bitmap identities and retires/reuploads textures; retirement
currently calls vkDeviceWaitIdle. This is a plausible cause, not measured churn
in that interrupted capture (its frame-build counters were zero).

Converted revised hull, steel, containers, coal and both equipment families,
including their glow/shadow/shade variants, to frame-sized PNG packs. Full sheets
remain authoring inputs only; native runtime file selection points at packs.
All 64 poses come from rigid geometry, with fixed waterline pivots and cargo
coaming depth. Entire revised catalog is 2432 frames / 192 MiB decoded, before
host/driver copies; each level prepares only its manifest, not that catalog.
Tutorial rotations expand from 1632 to 2080. Source and cropped-frame counters
must be considered together: moving frames into source storage is not by itself
a measured memory saving. Budget remains 160 MiB pending native measurement.

Android now removes previous-plan-only decoded sources/atlases and cropped cells
at a manifest change, preserving sources shared with the new plan. Previously
old cropped frames merely became evictable and source sheets stayed resident.
GPU textures still have their separate bounded LRU. Native validation pending.

First valid post-change native capture:
`out/profiles/20261121-034311-barge-abilities-freeze` (Vulkan, 90 warmup/120
measured frames, completed without interruption). All 2122 preparation frames
ready. Source storage 290.71 MiB plus cropped-frame storage 55.75 MiB = 346.46
MiB. The earlier interrupted run reported 429.69 MiB combined, but is not a
valid timing baseline. Warm frames built/evicted zero textures or raster layers.
Median draw 75.73 ms, interval 77.61 ms; max interval103.06 ms. Native Vulkan
log reported approximately3.72 ms GPU/3.84 ms encode during its120-frame window.
Thus there is ample crop-cache headroom now, but substantial CPU work remains;
do not claim a frame-rate fix. Captured pixels show both specialized barges,
registered new hulls, lighting, bridge/shore overlap and spray/fireworks.

  with original ring drawing restored.

### 2026-11-22 — Shared land scenery surface lighting
- Worktree codex/land-scenery-lighting from a88f775. All ArticulatedModel faces
  now receive world-space point lights, including procedural surface materials.
  SurfaceMaterial.LIT carries the already-graded face color through every host;
  Canvas/Vulkan/Metal strip the flag only when choosing the original material
  shader. Reference/desktop/Core Graphics use the same supplied color. UVs,
  alpha, wetness, BSP depth slices, building fades and emissive lenses survive.
- SceneryLighting owns one bounded thread-local frame snapshot (2,048 sources,
  2,048 spatial hash heads, 256-unit cells). Nine nearby cell queries per face,
  no per-face source collection/allocations or new textures. Production refreshes
  once per frame. Normals and centroids use physical XYZ; slicing uses the
  original face centroid so particle/rope depth cuts do not shift illumination.
- Sources include vessels/instructor/feeders/traffic, dock lamps, navigation
  beacons, exact moving bridge/crane/lock anchors, yard equipment, building
  windows and fire. Offscreen bridges are rejected before live light transforms.
  Worker arm/leg/lean transforms supply real face normals. Yard vehicles/benches,
  marina pedestal bodies and limestone revetment also receive surface lighting.
- Shared bridge/crew night previews reviewed: red signals illuminate steel,
  warm house lamps reach foundations, and nearby fires light shore spectators.
  Added normal/backface/physical-height, alpha, textured/flat receiver, geometry
  stability, emissive exclusion and Vulkan prelit-material encoding checks.

### 2026-11-22 — Live close-view marina boats
- Android baseline, montrose-haulout live, 120 warmup +180 measured frames:
  draw p50/p95 13.904/16.037 ms; end-to-end 17.185/20.852 ms. Capture:
  out/profiles/20261122-224923-montrose-haulout-live. No cold decoding/evictions.
- Added six prepared local BSP models, continuous rigid projection and real
  face-normal lighting. Thread-local geometry scratch is bounded by prepared
  fragment size, with no dynamic BSP rebuild or heading table. Geometry clips
  at the water plane and against foreground land. Nearby/full-size hulls use
  live geometry; distant/small hulls retain sprite LOD and normal lighting.
- Removed sailboat canopies/supports in both live geometry and all sprite poses;
  regenerated registered normals/canvas masks. Added 774-heading continuity,
  normalized normals, bounded topology, LOD and render-purity checks.
- First native live-model pass regressed: draw 23.643/27.050 ms and intervals
  28.913/33.810 ms (p50/p95), capture 20261122-225628-montrose-haulout-live.
  Thirty boats qualified, including a broad dual-path transition. Before shipping,
  narrowed distance fade to 180–210 and cached lighting once per original face
  per draw, not once per BSP fragment. Original-face centers keep highlights
  stable across topology traversal. Added exclusion 512 and profiling script
  BARGEBOSS_PROFILE_EXCLUDE for same-build sprite/live comparisons.
- Same-build A/B after face-light reuse: sprites draw 14.090/16.167 ms, interval
  17.436/20.055; models draw 18.364/22.203, interval 22.306/27.314 (p50/p95).
  Captures 20261122-230347 and 230405. A sampling trace then identified actual
  overhead: ShoreOcclusion/MontroseHarbor.shore 31.29% inclusive CPU,
  MontroseHarbor.disjoint 28.55%, MooredBoats.draw total 38.99%. This is hotspot
  attribution, not exact isolated invocation timing. Trace capture 230704.
- Vulkan's existing counting stencil was advertising no union support, forcing
  redundant CPU polygon subtraction for each mask. Enabled union capability
  with conservative 8-bit overlap budgeting and an exact shared disjoint fallback
  for extreme masks. Added 64-deep/1000-duplicate overflow/restoration checks.
  Kept the 180–210 live range; did not ship the proposed shorter 140–180 range.
- Initial union guard was too conservative: triangle count sent large already-
  sparse water masks back to ClipUnion (55.05% sampled CPU in trace 231649).
  Replaced it with conservative X/Y interval-overlap bounds using reusable
  sorted buffers. Added a 1000-separated-triangle fast-path test while retaining
  the 1000-overlap/64-level overflow test. Full shared/asset checks and 4,350,443
  Montrose shoreline pixels had passed; this follow-up is native encoder only,
  so rebuilt APK/native checks without repeating unchanged shared checks.
- Final same-build native A/B (180 sample frames after 120 warmup): sprites
  draw p50/p95 10.079/11.076 ms, intervals 16.604/25.431; live models draw
  12.740/13.916 ms, intervals 16.206/21.852. Captures 20261122-231957 and
  232011. Live geometry adds about 2.66 ms median drawing; the prior large
  regression was mask preparation. Long frames remain; these are application
  intervals, not a claim of perfectly locked GPU presentation at 60 FPS.
- Full shared checks, regenerated normal/canvas registration, Android build,
  native overflow/restore checks, and all-heading Montrose shoreline pixel
  regression passed. Reviewed native Vulkan scene for shoreline/boat occlusion.
  Replay 98c0ecc6:2:1350. Installed on Android; iOS hardware was not tested.
- Day/dusk review renders copied to Pictures/BargeBoss and MediaStore verified
  (18144/18145). Current boat materials remain flat face shading: the requested
